DEVOPS GLOSSARY CATEGORY CONTENT REVIEW


========== cicd-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

type Entry = {
  te: string;
  en: string;
  commands?: string[];
  example?: string;
  details?: string[];
};

const headings = [
  "1. Purpose – Why Is It Used?",
  "2. How It Works",
  "3. Real-Time Project Example",
  "4. Architecture / Components",
  "5. Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "6. Practical Commands",
  "7. Configuration Examples",
  "8. Common Mistakes",
  "9. Troubleshooting",
  "10. Best Practices",
  "11. Security Considerations",
  "12. Interview Points",
  "13. Related Concepts"
];

const r = (te: string, en: string, commands?: string[], example?: string, details?: string[]): Entry => ({
  te, en,
  ...(commands ? { commands } : {}),
  ...(example ? { example } : {}),
  ...(details ? { details } : {})
});

const makeSections = (concept: string, rows: Entry[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] => {
  if (rows.length !== 13) {
    throw new Error(`${concept}: expected 13 entries, received ${rows.length}`);
  }
  return rows.map((item, index) => ({
    title: headings[index],
    items: [{
      title: `${concept} – ${headings[index].replace(/^\d+\.\s*/, "")}`,
      explanation: `Telugu Answer:\n${item.te}\n\nEnglish Explanation:\n${item.en}`,
      ...(item.commands ? { commands: item.commands } : {}),
      ...(item.example ? { example: item.example } : {}),
      ...(item.details ? { details: item.details } : {})
    }]
  }));
};

========== cloud-aws-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

const r = (text: string) => text;

const makeSections = (rows: string[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] => {
  const titles = [
    "Definition",
    "Purpose and Importance",
    "How It Works",
    "Core Components",
    "Architecture",
    "Real-Time Project Example",
    "Practical Workflow",
    "Commands and Configuration",
    "Security Considerations",
    "High Availability and Reliability",
    "Monitoring and Troubleshooting",
    "Common Mistakes",
    "Best Practices",
  ];

  if (rows.length !== titles.length) {
    throw new Error(`Expected ${titles.length} AWS learning sections, received ${rows.length}`);
  }

  return rows.map((explanation, index) => ({
    title: titles[index],
    items: [{
      title: titles[index],
      explanation,
    }],
  }));
};

export const cloudAwsCategoryLearningContent: Record<string, GlossaryLearningSection[]> = {
  "Cloud Computing": makeSections([
    r("Cloud computing is the delivery of computing resources such as servers, storage, databases, networking and software over a network. Instead of purchasing and maintaining physical hardware, an organization consumes resources from a cloud provider and pays according to the selected pricing model."),
    r("Cloud computing helps teams provision infrastructure quickly, scale applications based on demand, reduce hardware maintenance and improve delivery speed. DevOps teams use it to create repeatable development, testing, staging and production environments."),
    r("A user selects a cloud service, configures its capacity and access, and provisions it through a console, CLI or Infrastructure as Code. The provider operates the underlying data centers while the customer remains responsible for configuring and securing the services they use."),
    r("Common service models include Infrastructure as a Service, Platform as a Service and Software as a Service. Deployment models include public, private and hybrid cloud. AWS provides services for compute, storage, networking, databases, security and monitoring."),
    r("A cloud architecture combines regions, availability zones, virtual networks, compute instances, storage, identity policies and monitoring. Applications can be distributed across multiple zones and connected through controlled network paths."),
    r("A company hosts a web application on Amazon EC2, stores uploaded files in Amazon S3, uses a managed database and places a load balancer in front of application instances. Infrastructure is provisioned using Terraform and deployments run through a CI/CD pipeline."),
    r("A typical workflow is to define infrastructure requirements, select suitable cloud services, create network and identity foundations, provision compute and storage, deploy the application, configure monitoring and continuously review security and cost."),

========== devsecops-security-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

const sectionTitles = [
  "Purpose - Why Is It Used?",
  "How It Works",
  "Real-Time Project Example",
  "Architecture / Components",
  "Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "Practical Commands",
  "Configuration Examples",
  "Common Mistakes",
  "Troubleshooting",
  "Best Practices",
  "Security Considerations",
  "Interview Points",
  "Related Concepts",
];

function r(telugu: string, english: string) {
  return { telugu, english };
}

function makeSections(rows: ReturnType<typeof r>[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] {
  return sectionTitles.map((title, index) => ({
    title,
    items: [{
      title: "Technical Explanation",
      explanation: rows[index].english,
      teluguExplanation: rows[index].telugu,
    }],
  }));
}

export const devsecopsSecurityCategoryLearningContent: Record<string, GlossaryLearningSection[]> = {
  "Role": makeSections([
    r("Role ante user or service account ki assign chese permissions collection. Role dwara e resources access cheyyachu, e actions perform cheyyachu ani define chestaru.", "A role is a collection of permissions assigned to a user, group, or service identity. It defines which resources can be accessed and which actions are allowed."),
    r("User ki direct permissions ivvakunda role assign chestaru. User role assume or receive chesinappudu aa role permissions effective avuthayi.", "Instead of granting permissions individually, administrators assign a role. When an identity receives or assumes that role, its associated permissions become effective."),
    r("CI/CD deployment service account ki Kubernetes deployment role assign chestaru. Appudu pipeline required namespace lo deployments update cheyyagaladu, kani cluster-wide admin access undadu.", "A CI/CD service account receives a deployment role that allows it to update workloads in a specific Kubernetes namespace without granting cluster-wide administrator access."),
    r("Role-based access lo identity, role definition, permissions, resource scope, trust policy, and assignment mechanism important components.", "Role-based access involves an identity, role definition, permissions, resource scope, trust policy, and an assignment mechanism."),
    r("Required actions identify cheyyali, role create chesi minimum permissions define cheyyali, trusted identities assign chesi access test and audit cheyyali.", "Identify required actions, create a role with minimum permissions, assign trusted identities, and test and audit the resulting access."),
    r("AWS IAM roles kosam aws iam get-role and aws iam list-attached-role-policies commands use cheyyachu. Kubernetes lo kubectl describe role and kubectl get rolebindings use cheyyachu.", "For AWS IAM, commands such as aws iam get-role and list-attached-role-policies inspect roles. Kubernetes supports kubectl describe role and kubectl get rolebindings."),
    r("IAM role policy lo Effect, Action, Resource, and optional Condition fields define chestaru. Kubernetes Role lo rules section apiGroups, resources, and verbs specify chestundi.", "An IAM role policy commonly defines Effect, Action, Resource, and optional Condition fields. A Kubernetes Role uses rules containing API groups, resources, and verbs."),
    r("Broad administrator permissions ivvadam, unused roles retain cheyyadam, and trust policy ni validate cheyyakapovadam common mistakes.", "Common mistakes include granting broad administrator permissions, retaining unused roles, and failing to validate role trust policies."),
    r("Access denied vaste assigned role, attached policies, resource scope, trust relationship, and explicit deny statements inspect cheyyali.", "When access is denied, inspect the assigned role, attached policies, resource scope, trust relationship, and any explicit deny statements."),
    r("Separate roles for deployment, monitoring, and administration maintain cheyyali. Permissions ni periodic ga review chesi unused access remove cheyyali.", "Maintain separate roles for deployment, monitoring, and administration. Review permissions periodically and remove access that is no longer needed."),
    r("Role assumption trusted principals ki matrame allow cheyyali. Cross-account trust and service identities ni carefully restrict cheyyali.", "Allow role assumption only for trusted principals. Carefully restrict cross-account trust relationships and service identities."),
    r("Role permissions define chestundi; identity evaru ane danini identify chestundi. Role-based access management permissions ni reusable and manageable ga chestundi.", "A role defines permissions, while an identity represents who or what is requesting access. Roles make permission management reusable and consistent."),
    r("IAM, RBAC, Authentication, Authorization, Least Privilege, Service Account, and Access Policy related concepts.", "Related concepts include IAM, RBAC, authentication, authorization, least privilege, service accounts, and access policies."),
  ]),

  "Encryption": makeSections([
    r("Encryption ante readable plaintext ni cryptographic algorithm and key use chesi unreadable ciphertext ga convert cheyyadam. ========== docker-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

const bi = (te: string, en: string) =>
  `Telugu Answer:\n${te}\n\nEnglish Explanation:\n${en}`;

type Entry = {
  title: string;
  te: string;
  en: string;
  commands?: string[];
  details?: string[];
  example?: string;
};

const sections = (
  name: string,
  entries: Entry[]
): GlossaryLearningSection[] =>
  entries.map((e, i) => ({
    title: [
      "1. Purpose – Why Is It Used?",
      "2. How It Works",
      "3. Real-Time Project Example",
      "4. Architecture / Components",
      "5. Step-by-Step Workflow",
      "6. Practical Commands",
      "7. Configuration Examples",
      "8. Common Mistakes",
      "9. Troubleshooting",
      "10. Best Practices",
      "11. Security Considerations",
      "12. Interview Points",
      "13. Related Concepts"
    ][i],
    items: [{
      title: e.title,
      explanation: bi(e.te, e.en),
      ...(e.commands ? { commands: e.commands } : {}),
      ...(e.details ? { details: e.details } : {}),
      ...(e.example ? { example: e.example } : {})
    }]
  }));

export const dockerCategoryLearningContent:
  Record<string, GlossaryLearningSection[]> = {

========== fundamentals-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

const sectionTitles = [
  "Purpose - Why Is It Used?",
  "How It Works",
  "Real-Time Project Example",
  "Architecture / Components",
  "Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "Practical Commands",
  "Configuration Examples",
  "Common Mistakes",
  "Troubleshooting",
  "Best Practices",
  "Security Considerations",
  "Interview Points",
  "Related Concepts",
];

function r(telugu: string, english: string) {
  return { telugu, english };
}

function makeSections(rows: ReturnType<typeof r>[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] {
  return sectionTitles.map((title, index) => ({
    title,
    items: [{
      title: "Technical Explanation",
      explanation: rows[index].english,
      teluguExplanation: rows[index].telugu,
    }],
  }));
}

export const fundamentalsCategoryLearningContent: Record<string, GlossaryLearningSection[]> = {
  "DevOps": makeSections([
    r("DevOps ante Development and Operations teams madhya collaboration improve chesi software ni faster, reliable ga deliver cheyyadaniki use chese culture, practices, and tools.", "DevOps combines cultural practices, processes, and tools to improve collaboration between software development and IT operations and deliver software more reliably."),
    r("Developers code changes create chestaru. CI pipelines changes validate chestayi. CD automation artifacts deploy chestundi.     r("Secrets Git history lo commit cheyyakudadhu.     r("Git distributed version control system. Commit recorded change. Branch independent line of development. Remote shared repository reference.", "Git is a distributed version control system. ========== git-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

type Entry = { te: string; en: string; commands?: string[]; example?: string };

const headings = [
  "1. Purpose – Why Is It Used?",
  "2. How It Works",
  "3. Real-Time Project Example",
  "4. Architecture / Components",
  "5. Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "6. Practical Commands",
  "7. Configuration Examples",
  "8. Common Mistakes",
  "9. Troubleshooting",
  "10. Best Practices",
  "11. Security Considerations",
  "12. Interview Points",
  "13. Related Concepts"
];

const r = (te: string, en: string, commands?: string[], example?: string): Entry => ({
  te, en,
  ...(commands ? { commands } : {}),
  ...(example ? { example } : {})
});

function makeSections(name: string, rows: Entry[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] {
  if (rows.length !== 13) throw new Error(`${name}: expected 13 sections, got ${rows.length}`);
  return rows.map((x, i) => ({
    title: headings[i],
    items: [{
      title: `${name} – ${headings[i].replace(/^\d+\.\s*/, "")}`,
      explanation: `Telugu Answer:\n${x.te}\n\nEnglish Explanation:\n${x.en}`,
      ...(x.commands ? { commands: x.commands } : {}),
      ...(x.example ? { example: x.example } : {})
    }]
  }));
}

export const gitCategoryLearningContent: Record<string, GlossaryLearningSection[]> = {

========== gitops-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

const sectionTitles = [
  "Purpose - Why Is It Used?",
  "How It Works",
  "Real-Time Project Example",
  "Architecture / Components",
  "Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "Practical Commands",
  "Configuration Examples",
  "Common Mistakes",
  "Troubleshooting",
  "Best Practices",
  "Security Considerations",
  "Interview Points",
  "Related Concepts",
];

function r(telugu: string, english: string) {
  return { telugu, english };
}

function makeSections(rows: ReturnType<typeof r>[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] {
  return sectionTitles.map((title, index) => ({
    title,
    items: [{
      title: "Technical Explanation",
      explanation: rows[index].english,
      teluguExplanation: rows[index].telugu,
    }],
  }));
}

export const gitopsCategoryLearningContent: Record<string, GlossaryLearningSection[]> = {
  "Argo CD": makeSections([
    r("Argo CD Kubernetes applications ni Git repository lo unna desired configuration prakaram deploy and synchronize cheyyadaniki use chestaru.", "Argo CD is a declarative GitOps continuous delivery tool for Kubernetes. It monitors Git repositories and reconciles cluster resources toward the desired configuration."),
    r("Argo CD repository configuration ni fetch chesi Kubernetes manifests render chestundi, live cluster state tho compare chesi differences identify chestundi.", "Argo CD retrieves application configuration from Git, renders Kubernetes manifests, compares desired resources with live cluster objects, and reports synchronization status."),
    r("Production team application manifests Git lo update chestundi. Argo CD change detect chesi approval policy prakaram Kubernetes cluster lo deployment synchronize chestundi.", "A production team updates a deployment image tag in Git. Argo CD detects the commit, renders the manifests, and synchronizes the approved application configuration to the target cluster."),
    r("Argo CD architecture lo API server, repository server, application controller, Redis cache, repo credentials, and Kubernetes cluster connections untayi.", "Argo CD includes an API server, repository server, application controller, Redis cache, repository credentials, and connections to managed Kubernetes clusters."),
    r("Git repository connect cheyyali, application define cheyyali, source path and destination cluster set cheyyali, sync status monitor chesi health verify cheyyali.", "Connect a Git repository, define an Argo CD Application with source and destination settings, inspect generated manifests, synchronize when authorized, and verify application health."),
    r("Commands: argocd login, argocd app list, argocd app get APP, argocd app diff APP, argocd app sync APP.", "Useful commands include argocd login, argocd app list, argocd app get, argocd app diff, and argocd app sync. Review differences before synchronization."),

========== iac-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

const sectionTitles = [
  "Purpose - Why Is It Used?",
  "How It Works",
  "Real-Time Project Example",
  "Architecture / Components",
  "Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "Practical Commands",
  "Configuration Examples",
  "Common Mistakes",
  "Troubleshooting",
  "Best Practices",
  "Security Considerations",
  "Interview Points",
  "Related Concepts",
];

function r(telugu: string, english: string) {
  return { telugu, english };
}

function makeSections(rows: ReturnType<typeof r>[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] {
  return sectionTitles.map((title, index) => ({
    title,
    items: [{
      title: "Technical Explanation",
      explanation: rows[index].english,
      teluguExplanation: rows[index].telugu,
    }],
  }));
}

export const iacCategoryLearningContent: Record<string, GlossaryLearningSection[]> = {
  "Infrastructure as Code": makeSections([
    r("Infrastructure ni manual ga create cheyyakunda code files dwara define chesi repeatable ga provision cheyyadam IaC.", "Infrastructure as Code defines servers, networks, storage, and cloud resources in machine-readable files so environments can be created consistently and repeatedly."),
    r("IaC engine configuration files ni read chesi desired infrastructure state ni provider APIs dwara actual resources ga convert chestundi.", "An IaC tool reads configuration, compares desired state with available state, and calls provider APIs to create, update, or remove infrastructure."),
    r("Team development, testing, staging, production environments kosam same infrastructure pattern ni version-controlled code nunchi deploy chestundi.", "A team defines a VPC, subnets, security rules, and compute resources once, then uses reviewed code and environment-specific inputs to create consistent development and production environments."),
    r("IaC lo configuration files, providers, state data, modules, variables, outputs, and execution engine important components.", "Typical IaC architecture includes configuration files, providers, reusable modules, input variables, state or inventory data, execution plans, and cloud or infrastructure APIs."),
    r("Requirements identify chesi resources define cheyyali, code review cheyyali, plan inspect chesi apply cheyyali, tarvata infrastructure verify cheyyali.", "The workflow starts with infrastructure requirements, defines resources in code, validates and reviews changes, previews the execution plan, applies approved changes, and verifies the resulting environment."),
    r("Terraform workflow lo terraform init, terraform fmt, terraform validate, terraform plan, terraform apply commands use chestaru.", "Common Terraform commands include terraform init to initialize providers, terraform fmt to format files, terraform validate to check configuration, terraform plan to preview changes, and terraform apply to execute them."),
    r("Example ga VPC, subnet, security group, EC2 resources ni HCL files lo define chesi variables dwara region and instance size control cheyyachu.", "A typical configuration defines a VPC, subnet, security group, and EC2 instance in HCL, while variables control region, instance size, and environment-specific values."),
    r("Manual infrastructure changes, hardcoded credentials, missing state backup, and unreviewed applies environment differences and outages create chestayi.", "Common failures include configuration drift, hardcoded secrets, missing state protection, overly broad permissions, unreviewed changes, and inconsistent provider versions."),
    r("Resource create avvakapothe plan output, provider authentication, API permissions, quotas, dependency order, and state consistency inspect cheyyali.", "Troubleshooting should examine validation errors, provider authentication, API permissions, resource quotas, dependency references, state consistency, and the exact plan output."),

    r("Ansible Playbook, Role Dependency, Variable Precedence, Handler, Ansible Galaxy, and Inventory related concepts.", "Related concepts include playbooks, role dependencies, variable precedence, handlers, Ansible Galaxy, inventory, and Ansible Vault."),
  ]),
};


========== kubernetes-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

type Row = {
  title: string;
  te: string;
  en: string;
  example?: string;
  commands?: string[];
  details?: string[];
};

const sectionTitles = [
  "1. Purpose – Why Is It Used?",
  "2. How It Works",
  "3. Real-Time Project Example",
  "4. Architecture / Components",
  "5. Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "6. Practical Commands",
  "7. Configuration Examples",
  "8. Common Mistakes",
  "9. Troubleshooting",
  "10. Best Practices",
  "11. Security Considerations",
  "12. Interview Points",
  "13. Related Concepts"
];

const makeSections = (rows: Row[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] => {
  if (rows.length !== 13) {
    throw new Error(`Expected 13 learning entries, received ${rows.length}`);
  }

  return rows.map((r, i) => ({
    title: sectionTitles[i],
    items: [{
      title: r.title,
      explanation: `Telugu Answer:\n${r.te}\n\nEnglish Explanation:\n${r.en}`,
      ...(r.example ? { example: r.example } : {}),
      ...(r.commands ? { commands: r.commands } : {}),
      ...(r.details ? { details: r.details } : {})
    }]
  }));
};

========== linux-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

const sectionTitles = [
  "Purpose - Why Is It Used?",
  "How It Works",
  "Real-Time Project Example",
  "Architecture / Components",
  "Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "Practical Commands",
  "Configuration Examples",
  "Common Mistakes",
  "Troubleshooting",
  "Best Practices",
  "Security Considerations",
  "Interview Points",
  "Related Concepts",
];

function r(telugu: string, english: string) {
  return { telugu, english };
}

function makeSections(rows: ReturnType<typeof r>[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] {
  return sectionTitles.map((title, index) => ({
    title,
    items: [{
      title: "Technical Explanation",
      explanation: rows[index].english,
      teluguExplanation: rows[index].telugu,
    }],
  }));
}

export const linuxCategoryLearningContent: Record<string, GlossaryLearningSection[]> = {

========== monitoring-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

const sectionTitles = [
  "Purpose - Why Is It Used?",
  "How It Works",
  "Real-Time Project Example",
  "Architecture / Components",
  "Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "Practical Commands",
  "Configuration Examples",
  "Common Mistakes",
  "Troubleshooting",
  "Best Practices",
  "Security Considerations",
  "Interview Points",
  "Related Concepts",
];

function r(telugu: string, english: string) {
  return { telugu, english };
}

function makeSections(rows: ReturnType<typeof r>[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] {
  return sectionTitles.map((title, index) => ({
    title,
    items: [{
      title: "Technical Explanation",
      explanation: rows[index].english,
      teluguExplanation: rows[index].telugu,
    }],
  }));
}

export const monitoringCategoryLearningContent: Record<string, GlossaryLearningSection[]> = {
  "Metrics": makeSections([
    r("Metrics ante application, infrastructure, and system performance ni numbers form lo measure chese measurements. CPU usage, memory usage, request count examples.", "Metrics are numerical measurements that describe the behavior and performance of applications, infrastructure, and services. Examples include CPU utilization, memory consumption, request rate, and error count."),
    r("Monitoring agent or application metrics collect chestundi. Prometheus lanti system timestamp and labels tho samples store chesi queries and alerts ki use chestundi.", "Applications or exporters expose metric samples. A monitoring system collects them with timestamps and labels, stores time-series data, and makes it available for queries, dashboards, and alerts."),
    r("Production API lo request rate, error rate, and latency metrics monitor chesi release tarvata performance degradation identify chestaru.", "A production API team monitors request rate, error rate, and latency to detect whether a new release increases failures or slows down user requests."),
    r("Metric architecture lo instrumentation, exporter, scrape or collection process, time-series database, query engine, dashboard, and alert rules untayi.", "A metrics pipeline commonly includes application instrumentation, exporters, collection or scraping, time-series storage, a query engine, dashboards, and alert evaluation."),
    r("Important service indicators select cheyyali, metrics expose cheyyali, collector configure cheyyali, dashboards build chesi meaningful alert thresholds define cheyyali.", "Identify useful service indicators, instrument the application or exporter, configure collection, build dashboards, and define alerts with clear thresholds and ownership."),
    r("Prometheus lo up, rate(http_requests_total[5m]), and process_cpu_seconds_total queries use chesi metrics inspect cheyyachu.", "Prometheus queries such as up, rate(http_requests_total[5m]), and process_cpu_seconds_total help inspect target availability, request rates, and process CPU time."),

========== production-reliability-category-learning-content.ts ==========
import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

const sectionTitles = [
  "Purpose - Why Is It Used?",
  "How It Works",
  "Real-Time Project Example",
  "Architecture / Components",
  "Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "Practical Commands",
  "Configuration Examples",
  "Common Mistakes",
  "Troubleshooting",
  "Best Practices",
  "Security Considerations",
  "Interview Points",
  "Related Concepts",
];

function r(telugu: string, english: string) {
  return { telugu, english };
}

function makeSections(rows: ReturnType<typeof r>[]): GlossaryLearningSection[] {
  return sectionTitles.map((title, index) => ({
    title,
    items: [{
      title: "Technical Explanation",
      explanation: rows[index].english,
      teluguExplanation: rows[index].telugu,
    }],
  }));
}

export const productionReliabilityCategoryLearningContent: Record<string, GlossaryLearningSection[]> = {
  "Scalability": makeSections([
    r("Scalability ante workload increase ayina application or infrastructure capacity ni increase chesi acceptable performance maintain cheyyadam.", "Scalability is the ability of a system to handle increased workload by adding or reallocating resources while maintaining acceptable performance."),
    r("Vertical scaling lo existing server ki CPU or memory add chestaru. Horizontal scaling lo additional instances or nodes add chesi traffic distribute chestaru.", "Vertical scaling increases resources on an existing machine. Horizontal scaling adds more instances or nodes and distributes workload across them."),
    r("E-commerce traffic sale time lo increase ayithe application replicas and worker capacity scale out chesi additional requests handle chestaru.", "During an e-commerce sale, an application can add replicas and worker capacity to handle increased traffic and background processing."),
    r("Scalability architecture lo load balancer, stateless application replicas, database capacity, cache, queues, autoscaler, and monitoring untayi.", "A scalable architecture may include load balancers, stateless application replicas, database capacity, caching, message queues, autoscaling, and monitoring."),
    r("Baseline traffic measure cheyyali, bottleneck identify cheyyali, scaling strategy choose chesi load test perform chesi capacity policies tune cheyyali.", "Measure baseline traffic, identify bottlenecks, select a scaling strategy, perform load tests, and tune capacity policies using observed behavior."),
    r("Linux lo top, htop, free -h, vmstat, and iostat resource usage inspect cheyyadaniki use avuthayi.     r("Kubernetes lo Deployment replicas and Horizontal Pod Autoscaler configure cheyyachu. Set realistic resource requests and limits."),
    r("Application statelessness ignore cheyyadam, database bottleneck consider cheyyakapovadam, and load test lekunda autoscaling configure cheyyadam common mistakes.", "Common mistakes include relying on stateful application replicas, ignoring database bottlenecks, and enabling autoscaling without representative load testing."),
    r("Scaling jarigina performance improve kakapothe CPU, memory, database connections, queue depth, network latency, and downstream service limits inspect cheyyali.", "If scaling does not improve performance, inspect CPU, memory, database connections, queue depth, network latency, and downstream service capacity."),
    r("Capacity planning, load testing, caching, stateless design, graceful degradation, and scaling limits implement cheyyali.", "Use capacity planning, realistic load testing, caching, stateless design, graceful degradation, and safe minimum and maximum scaling limits."),
    r("Uncontrolled scaling cost increase cheyyachu. Autoscaling permissions restrict cheyyali and resource limits and budgets monitor cheyyali.", "Uncontrolled scaling can increase cost and attack impact. Restrict autoscaling permissions and monitor resource limits, quotas, and budgets."),

  "Fault Tolerance": makeSections([
    r("Fault tolerance ante individual component fail ayina complete system service continue cheyyagalige design capability.", "Fault tolerance is the ability of a system to continue providing an acceptable level of service when one or more components fail."),
    r("Redundant components, health checks, automatic failover, replication, and failure isolation use chesi single component dependency reduce chestaru.", "Fault-tolerant systems reduce single-component dependencies through redundancy, health checks, failover, replication, and failure isolation."),

export type GlossaryLearningSection = {
  title: string;
    teluguTitle?: string;
  items: {
    title: string;
    teluguTitle?: string;
    explanation: string;
    teluguExplanation?: string;
    example?: string;
    teluguExample?: string;
    details?: string[];
    teluguDetails?: string[];
    commands?: string[];
  }[];
};
export type GlossaryTerm = {
  name: string;
  definition: string;
  purpose: string;
  project: string;
  workflow: string[];
  commands: string[];
  mistakes: string[];
  related: string[];
  configuration?: string[];
  troubleshooting?: string[];
  bestPractices?: string[];
  securityConsiderations?: string[];
  learningContent?: GlossaryLearningSection[];
};

export type GlossaryCategory = {
  slug: string;
  title: string;
    teluguTitle?: string;
  description: string;
  purpose: string;
  icon: string;
  terms: GlossaryTerm[];
};

const makeTerms = (
  entries: [string, string, string, string[]][]
): GlossaryTerm[] =>
  entries.map(([name, definition, project, related]) => ({
    name,
    definition,

export function slugify(value: string): string {
  return value
    .toLowerCase()
    .replace(/&/g, "and")
    .replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, "-")
    .replace(/^-|-$/g, "");
}

export function getCategory(slug: string) {
  return glossaryCategories.find((category) => category.slug === slug);
}

export function getTerm(categorySlug: string, termSlug: string) {
  const category = getCategory(categorySlug);
  return category?.terms.find((term) => slugify(term.name) === termSlug);
}

export function getAllTermParams() {
  return glossaryCategories.flatMap((category) =>
    category.terms.map((term) => ({
      category: category.slug,
      term: slugify(term.name),
    }))
  );
}



























import type { GlossaryLearningSection } from "./glossary-data";

const bilingual = (telugu: string, english: string) =>
  `Telugu Answer:\n${telugu}\n\nEnglish Explanation:\n${english}`;

const commandMap: Record<string, string[]> = {
  "Docker": ["docker version", "docker info", "docker ps -a", "docker images"],
  "Kubernetes": ["kubectl get nodes", "kubectl get pods -A", "kubectl describe pod <pod-name>", "kubectl get events -A"],
  "Jenkins": ["systemctl status jenkins", "journalctl -u jenkins -n 100", "curl -I http://localhost:8080"],
  "Git": ["git status", "git log --oneline --graph", "git branch -a", "git diff"],
  "Terraform": ["terraform init", "terraform validate", "terraform plan", "terraform state list"],
  "Ansible": ["ansible --version", "ansible-inventory --list", "ansible all -m ping", "ansible-playbook --syntax-check site.yml"],
  "Linux": ["ps aux", "top", "df -h", "free -m", "journalctl -xe"],
  "Prometheus": ["promtool check config prometheus.yml", "curl http://localhost:9090/-/healthy"],
  "Grafana": ["systemctl status grafana-server", "journalctl -u grafana-server -n 100"],
  "Helm": ["helm list -A", "helm lint ./chart", "helm template release ./chart", "helm history release"],
  "Argo CD": ["argocd app list", "argocd app get <app-name>", "argocd app diff <app-name>", "argocd app sync <app-name>"],
  "AWS": ["aws sts get-caller-identity", "aws configure list", "aws ec2 describe-instances"],
  "Maven": ["mvn validate", "mvn test", "mvn package"],
  "Nexus": ["curl -I https://<nexus-host>/service/rest/v1/status"],
  "GitHub Actions": ["gh workflow list", "gh run list", "gh run view <run-id>"],
  "default": ["<tool-specific-command> --help", "Check the service status", "Review application and service logs"]
};

function getCommands(name: string): string[] {
  const key = Object.keys(commandMap).find(k =>
    k !== "default" && name.toLowerCase().includes(k.toLowerCase())
  );
  return commandMap[key || "default"];
}

const sectionTitles = [
  "1. Purpose – Why Is It Used?",
  "2. How It Works",
  "3. Real-Time Project Example",
  "4. Architecture / Components",
  "5. Step-by-Step Workflow",
  "6. Practical Commands",
  "7. Configuration Examples",
  "8. Common Mistakes",
  "9. Troubleshooting",
  "10. Best Practices",
  "11. Security Considerations",
  "12. Interview Points",
  "13. Related Concepts"
];

export function buildBulkGlossaryLearningContent(
  name: string,
  definition: string,
  project: string,
  related: string[]
): GlossaryLearningSection[] | undefined {
  // Preserve the two completed learning-content pages exactly.
  if (name === "Docker Image" || name === "Docker Container") return undefined;

  const commands = getCommands(name);
  const projectContext = project || "a production DevOps project";
  const relatedText = related?.length ? related.join(", ") : "the surrounding DevOps workflow";

      title: "Purpose",

      en: `${name} is used to address a specific requirement in the DevOps lifecycle. ${definition} In ${projectContext}, teams use it where this capability is required instead of treating it as an isolated tool or concept.`,
      details: [
        `Primary responsibility: ${definition}`,
        "Identify the requirement before selecting configuration or implementation options."
      ]
    },
    {
      title: "How It Works",
      te: `${name} పని చేసే విధానం దాని configuration, inputs, dependencies మరియు environment పై ఆధారపడి ఉంటుంది. ముందుగా input లేదా request అందుతుంది. తర్వాత సంబంధిత processing జరుగుతుంది. చివరగా output లేదా status ను verify చేయాలి.`,
      en: `${name} operates through its configured inputs, dependencies and execution environment. A request or configuration is provided, the relevant component processes it, and the resulting state or output is checked. The exact implementation depends on the technology and its deployment model.`,
      details: [
        "Understand the input, processing stage and expected output.",
        "Check dependencies and permissions before execution.",
        "Validate the resulting state rather than assuming success."
      ]
    },
    {
      title: "Real-Time Project Example",

      en: `In ${projectContext}, the team uses ${name} at the point where its capability is needed. After a change, engineers validate the result through service status, logs, metrics and application behaviour. The implementation should match the actual project architecture.`,
      details: [
        "Use a development or staging environment to validate the change first.",
        "Record the expected result and compare it with the observed result."
      ]
    },
    {
      title: "Architecture / Components",
      te: `${name} ను అర్థం చేసుకోవడానికి దాని ప్రధాన components, వాటి responsibilities మరియు వాటి మధ్య communication తెలుసుకోవాలి. ప్రతి component role ను గుర్తించి, dependencies ఎక్కడ ఉన్నాయో పరిశీలించాలి.`,
      en: `The architecture around ${name} consists of the components that provide inputs, perform the operation, store or transfer state, and expose results. Identify each component's responsibility and the communication path between them before troubleshooting.`,

        "Identify the main component responsible for the operation.",

        "Review command output and exit codes before taking corrective action."
      ]

    {
      title: "Configuration Examples",
      te: `${name} configuration project requirements మరియు environment ఆధారంగా మారుతుంది. Development, staging మరియు production values ను వేరు చేయాలి. Credentials లేదా secrets ను source code లో hardcode చేయకూడదు.`,
      en: `Configuration for ${name} depends on the selected platform and project requirements. Keep environment-specific values separate, validate configuration syntax, and store credentials in an approved secrets-management system. The exact configuration file and keys must follow the technology's official specification.`,

      en: `Common mistakes include applying unvalidated configuration, overlooking dependencies, granting excessive permissions, and assuming that a successful command guarantees application health. These issues can cause failures that are difficult to diagnose.`,

      en: `Troubleshoot ${name} by collecting the exact error, identifying the affected component, checking service health and reviewing recent changes. Validate configuration, permissions, connectivity and dependencies. Apply one controlled correction at a time and verify recovery.`,
      details: [
        "Capture timestamps, error messages and recent change history.",

      en: `Follow consistent configuration standards for ${name}. Review changes, enable appropriate observability, document operational procedures, and prepare a rollback plan. Test upgrades and configuration changes before production rollout.`,
      details: [
        "Use version control for configuration where appropriate.",

        "Monitor the result and review operational alerts.",
        "Maintain a tested recovery or rollback procedure."

      te: `${name} ను secure గా ఉపయోగించడానికి అవసరమైన permissions మాత్రమే ఇవ్వాలి. Secrets ను protect చేయాలి. Access ను audit చేయాలి. Network communication మరియు stored data కు సరైన security controls అమలు చేయాలి.`,

      title: "Related Concepts",

    }]
  }));
